# Design Concerns & Decisions Pending (Bruce)

Living log, maintained by hand since 2026-09-16 — the daily 4pm council cron was retired (row 154). Items here are things **Bruce must rule on** —
design-flavored questions that cannot be resolved autonomously.

How it works:
- The walkthrough appends new rows (chapter, source lens, crisp question).
- Bruce answers in chat; Winston marks the row **resolved** with the ruling and files any pipeline work it implies.
- Never silently close a row. Never decide a design question in this file without Bruce's answer.

| 24 | 11-arcane-spells | 9 spells keyed to disciplines that exist in NO chapter: Cold (Frostbite Shard, Rime Storm, Glacial Prison), Lightning (Storm Javelin, Forked Sky, Static Field), Acid (Corrosive Spit, Caustic Mist), Poison (Venom Lance). Strong anchors: Cold->Water, Lightning->Wind. Judgment calls (most likely to veto): Acid->Earth and Poison->Plants. Veto to revert (e.g., re-key Acid/Poison, or add Cold/Lightning/Acid/Poison as real disciplines in ch08 if the 6-elemental system is wanted back). |

Which? | Council (Layout, ch13 day, Synod run 20260825-2251) | resolved 2026-09-10 (implemented, PR #461) | **Ruling 2026-09-10 (Bruce, verbatim intent): if the table cannot fit on a single page, split it and put headers on BOTH splits. "Always be creative with the layout to fit what the data is, don't brute force it."** Implementation order: (1) try relocation with a prose pointer; (2) if it still cannot fit, split with repeated headers. Theme change (b) NOT taken. |

Add this to the Deep Gash row (ch13:370) and make sure the death-save section says damage taken at 0 HP counts as a failed save. The 666 row remains the only outcome that bypasses survival rolls entirely. |

| 75 | book-wide (crossref rendering) | Every rendered chapter crossref in the book shows a chapter number one lower than the printed TOC and heading pages: the front-matter title chapter (index.qmd, TOC "Chapter 1: Heroes of Legend") is counted in the TOC/heading numbering but not in the Typst crossref counter, so all 78+ @sec-chapter-* links render off by one (verified against 8 anchors: opening-fiction renders 2 vs TOC 3, character-creation 3 vs 4, attributes 4 vs 5, core-resolution 7 vs 8, combat 14 vs 15, armor 17 vs 18, gm-guidance 20 vs 21, disciplines 9 vs 10; also confirmed present in the pre-ch01b build, so it is pre-existing, not introduced by the new ch01 links). | RULED 2026-09-10 - root cause located, fix pending | **Ruling 2026-09-10: fix it (defect, not a design choice).** DIAGNOSED precisely: the book carries THREE chapter numberings. (1) Printed TOC: front matter = Chapter 1, Introduction = 2, ... License = 25. (2) Chapter ornaments (roman): front matter = Chapter I, Introduction = II. These two AGREE. (3) Crossrefs (@sec-): Introduction = 1 ... Core Resolution = 7 - ONE LOW, and the outlier. Verified evidence: ch07:66 renders 'the full tier rules ... live in Chapter 7' while the TOC and the ornament both call those rules Chapter 8. ROOT CAUSE LOCATED: `.quarto/typst/packages/preview/orange-book/0.7.1/lib.typ:117` (and :143) executes `counter(heading).update(0)` at the start of every chapter. The front matter (index.qmd) renders its title through the template's chapter function WITHOUT emitting a level-1 heading, so the heading counter stays 0 for it, while the TOC and ornament number it from a separate chapter/part counter. FIX ATTEMPTS THAT FAILED (do not repeat): (a) seeding `counter(heading).update(1)` in index.qmd's typst block - build green, refs unchanged; (b) declaring a hidden level-1 heading (`#hide(heading(level:1, outlined:false, numbering:none)[...])`) - refs STILL unchanged AND it added a duplicate TOC entry ('Chapter 2: Heroes of Legend') and shifted the TOC, i.e. it made the book worse. Both reverted; tree clean. REMAINING PATH: fix at the template/counter level (give the front matter a real counted heading inside the chapter scope, or offset the crossref counter in the orange-book chapter function). Needs a render check of every @sec- site. Batch with #71 and #84 - same template surface. SIDE FIX SHIPPED: the doubled period this defect exposed in ch07 ('Chapter 7..') is fixed in PR #466 (the only `@sec-*.` site in the repo). | Not fixed chapter-locally (a per-chapter edit cannot change how the orange-book template numbers the front-matter chapter relative to the crossref counter; it would make one chapter inconsistent with the rest). Two fix directions: (a) make the front matter unnumbered so the crossref counter aligns with the TOC (TOC renumbers to start Introduction at Chapter 1), or (b) renumber the TOC/heading pages to match the crossref counter (Introduction = Chapter 2, etc.). Same family as #71 (front-matter rendering). The two new ch01 crossrefs (PR #394) are correct relative to the book's existing 78 links and do not worsen the offset. | **UMBRELLA: issue #467** (research + fix: unify chapter/page numbering) - #71, #75 and #84 are ONE template-level root cause (orange-book lib.typ:117/143 resets counter(heading) per chapter; the front matter renders a title with no level-1 heading). Solve there, once, with a full-book render check. Do NOT attempt further chapter-local fixes.

| 77 | book-wide (crossref rendering) | Bare cross-references followed by an author-typed period render a doubled full stop in the PDF: the Typst engine appends its own terminal period when resolving the integer chapter label, so `see @sec-chapter-core-resolution.` prints as "see Chapter 7..". The ch03 layout pass (f-007) found it at 03-attributes.qmd:43, but recon confirmed the pattern is BOOK-WIDE: 18 bare refs across 11 chapters (ch01 x6, ch02 x3, ch03 x1, ch05 x1, ch06 x1, ch08 x1, ch13 x2, ch15 x2, ch16 x2, ch18 x1, ch21 x1) all render "Chapter N.." in the built PDF, while the parenthetical form `(see @sec-...)` renders cleanly with a single period. Per the book-wide convention rule (ch13 2026-08-26), a single-chapter fix would make ch03 inconsistent with the rest of the book, so it was not shipped chapter-locally. Two fix directions: (A) parenthetical sweep - wrap each bare ref in parentheses and drop the author period, ~18 edits across 11 chapters, one PR per chapter group; or (B) accept the doubled period as a known render artifact and document it. | Layout/Design (ch03 day, run 20260907-2007; re-confirmed ch06 day 2026-09-10) | resolved (implemented 2026-09-10, PR #457) | IMPLEMENTED DEFAULT, third direction: neither (A) nor (B) - drop the AUTHOR's period and keep the reference's own, since the reference already renders its terminal period. No parenthetical rewrite, no documenting an artifact. Swept book-wide: 20 bare refs by script plus 2 follow-ups (a parenthetical `@sec-...).` and a new prose ref this run added), verified in the rebuilt PDF: `grep -c '\.\.'` on the rendered text drops from 21 to 0. NOTE: table references (`@tbl-*`) do NOT double - their author period is correct (`See Table 2.1.`) - so the fix is `@sec-` only. Veto to revert. |
| 78 | 04-ancestries-cultures | Ancestry gift tables (04:148-193) carry only Gift/Tier/Effect and list NO Discipline prerequisites, yet the gift intro (04:144) says they "follow the same card format as class abilities" (whose tables have a Disc Req column, e.g. 05-classes:451) and ch08's ladder requires Adept cards = exactly 2 and Master = exactly 3 Discipline prerequisites. Adding a prereq column would mean inventing 14 prereq assignments (5 gifts x 4 ancestries, minus the Novice basics) - a design-authority call, not a mechanical one. Two directions: (A) gifts are innate (bloodline, not learned), so they are EXEMPT from Discipline prerequisites - the tier then reflects cost/complexity, not prereq count; in which case 04:144's "same card format" line should be softened to "same tier ladder and cost, not the same column set"; or (B) gifts DO require Disciplines like any other ability - in which case 14 prereq cells must be authored (and the 2026-08-13 anti-power-creep ceiling rule still applies). The book has never stated which. | Council (EIC+Contrarian, ch04 day) | resolved 2026-09-10 (implemented, PR #461) | **Ruling 2026-09-10 (Bruce, verbatim): "the only ancestry requirement is the ancestry itself."** Direction (A): gifts are innate and EXEMPT from Discipline prerequisites; the ancestry is the sole requirement. Soften 04:144's "same card format as class abilities" to state the shared part (tier ladder and 2/4/8 cost) without implying a Disc Req column. Do NOT author 14 prereq cells. The anti-power-creep ceiling rule still applies unconditionally. |

| 95 | book-wide (core mechanics) | **PRINCIPLE (Bruce 2026-09-10): roll modifiers become Boon/Bane.** Numeric modifiers that shift a roll's likelihood contradict the near-zero-maths goal; Boon/Bane achieves a similar effect, resolves interaction problems, and leaves the final call to the DA. Numbers belong to DAMAGE. Recon found 58 numeric roll modifiers across 14 files, in four categories. | Bruce directive 2026-09-10 | resolved 2026-09-10 (implemented, PRs #470/#471) | **Ruling 2026-09-10 (Bruce): categories A and C stay numeric; only B converts.** KEEP numeric: (A) attributes and skill tiers (Novice +1 / Adept +2 / Master +3), because that is the character's competence and ch06 promises it matters more than luck; (C) the difficulty dial (Hard -2, Very Hard -4) and Challenge (-1..-6), because that is the DA's own dial. CONVERT to Boon/Bane: (B) the situational layer - maneuvers, wounds, ancestry/class benefits, spell riders, equipment penalties. (D) damage, HP, DR, DP, ranges and durations are untouched. |
| 96 | 06-core-resolution (book-wide) | **Boon/Bane potence ladder + cancellation.** The book defines a single Boon/Bane (4d6 keep highest/lowest 3) but nothing for double or triple, and no rule for what happens when a roll carries both. Without this the situational conversion (row 95, category B) is lossy, because graded modifiers such as armor's -1/-2/-3 spell penalty must map onto a ladder. | Bruce directive 2026-09-10 | resolved 2026-09-10 (implemented, PRs #470/#471) | **Ruling 2026-09-10 (Bruce): Boons and Banes CANCEL one for one; a roll with one of each is a straight 3d6 roll.** Implemented ladder (default, veto-able): Boon 1 = 4d6 keep highest 3; Boon 2 = 5d6; Boon 3 = 6d6; Banes mirror with keep-lowest. Cancel one-for-one BEFORE rolling, then the net step count sets the dice. Cap at three steps: potence beyond triple has no further effect (Bruce named double and triple specifically). Consequences: armor's -1/-2/-3 spell penalty maps to Bane / double Bane / triple Bane; ch06's "a Boon is worth roughly +2 on the roll" must be rewritten, since under this principle a Boon is not a disguised number. |
| 97 | 13-combat (book-wide) | **The tier-shift axis vs Boon/Bane.** A third mechanism exists beside numbers and Boon/Bane: the tier shift (Blinded "attacks reduced one tier"; Overchannel/Swift Blade/Death Blow "+1 damage tier"). It moves the OUTCOME BAND, not the roll's likelihood, so it was already number-free. | Bruce directive 2026-09-10 | resolved 2026-09-10 (implemented, PRs #470/#471) | **Ruling 2026-09-10 (Bruce): split the two uses.** Blinded becomes a DOUBLE BANE on attacks (a severe condition, and double Bane is the potence enabled in row 96), because a condition applies Boon/Bane. The DAMAGE-side tier shift ("+1 damage tier") is left UNTOUCHED as a distinct tool, since numbers belong to damage under this principle. Implemented default for other conditions: any condition currently phrased as a numeric roll modifier converts to Bane/double Bane; conditions already using Boon/Bane are already compliant. |

| 103 | 09-talents-abilities (weapon maneuvers) | **A multi-rank maneuver's base damage row: is it the row of its own tier, or always the Novice row?** The researcher lens read five damaging maneuvers as printing a row BELOW their tier (Riposte 2 Blades/Adept and Parry 2 Blades/Adept base 2/4/6; Shield Slam 2 Armor/Adept base 1/2/3; Cyclone 3 Blades/Master base 2/4/6; Brace 2 Polearms base 2/2/2) and the chapter's own ladder as proof of intent elsewhere. FOR that reading: 08:227-230 keys Damage to Discipline Requirement ("the table is the law"), 08:208 makes a two-rank card Adept, 08:122 (the prereq table) lists Shield Slam itself as the Adept example, 05:635/637/659 cite 12 (the Adept Strong) for Adept cards and 05:543/710 cite 9/15 (the Master rows) for Master cards, and every one of ch11/ch12's two-rank cards prints 6 as its base Weak (Brimstone Burst, Caustic Mist, Pillar of Light, Restoration, Touch of the Grave, and 8 more) while every one-rank card prints 2. AGAINST: 09:15 says the Weak/Standard/Strong block is "the Novice effect" for every active card, and this chapter's Wave-1 pass (PR #366, 2026-08-24) explicitly VERIFIED the opposite - its progress row records "Riposte 2/4/6->6/9/12->9/15/21, Cyclone/Cleave/Pinpoint/Charge/Crushing Blow/Executioner's Cut on-row, Renewal 2/4/6->6/9/12->9/15/21, Parry DR rows" as correct, and its Shield Slam fix moved only the Adept/Master lines (2/4/6->6/9/12, 6/9/12->9/15/21), deliberately leaving the 1/2/3 base. Also blocking a uniform fix: Cyclone carries three ranks, so under the tier-keyed reading its own Adept line (6/9/12) would sit BELOW its new base (9/15/21) and the card's ladder would collapse - that is a card-format decision, not a number tweak. | Council (Researcher f-003 + Librarian adjudication, ch09 day, Synod run 20260911-2024; disputed by Game Architect f-004 in-round) | **RULED 2026-09-12 (Bruce, Option A)** - maneuver base = the Novice row; spells key to their own tier. | **Shipped:** Shield Slam base 1/2/3 -> 2/4/6 (the only maneuver printing a non-row base) and the maneuver-vs-spell distinction stated in the Weapon Maneuvers intro, so both card families are now internally and mutually consistent. PR #488, merged 115f18d. Riposte/Parry/Brace/Renewal bases kept as verified by the Wave-1 pass. The wider tier-keyed reading was considered and rejected: it would overturn that earlier verification and collapse Cyclone's three-rank ladder. **CORRECTION 2026-09-12 (same day):** the shipped rationale first read "your weapon supplies the base damage", which contradicts the locked weapons-are-flavor law (ch15: "the weapon never changes these numbers"). Rewritten to the correct anchor - the base block IS the card's Novice rung, per 09:15 ("the Novice effect") - with the weapon contributing nothing. Same PR as #104. |
| 104 | 09-talents-abilities (Weapon Focus) | **"+2 damage tiers" / "+3 damage tiers" are the only multi-step tier bumps in the book** (09:61/63). Every other tier bump in six chapters is a single "+1 damage tier" (the Overchannel convention), and the design rulings define the sanctioned forms as a budget row, a Basic-card floor, or a "+1 damage tier" bump. The council's suggested mechanical fix is not executable: restating Adept as "+1 tier" would make it identical to Novice and leave a dead rung, so what the Adept and Master rungs BUY needs a designer's intent (a second attack? a wider effect? a cap?). Note the bump is self-capping at Strong under the ch15:220 rule, so this is about form and about the Master rung's power, not about exceeding the table. | Council (Game Architect f-004 + Researcher f-003, ch09 day, Synod run 20260911-2024) | **RULED 2026-09-12 (Bruce, REVISED)** - not a damage-tier bump at all: Weapon Focus grants a **Boon to the attack roll** with the chosen weapon Discipline, stepping up the potence ladder by talent tier. | **Shipped:** first verified the prerequisite - the "weapons are flavor, cards are damage" law is applied book-wide (ch15 states it, the weapon table carries no damage column, no weapon damage bonus survives anywhere). Bruce then revised the ruling: rather than legalising a multi-step "+2/+3 damage tiers" form, Weapon Focus grants Boon / Double Boon / Triple Boon on attack rolls with the chosen Discipline. This resolves #104 by DELETING the anomaly (no multi-step damage bump remains in the book) and obeys the locked 2026-09-10 no-math ruling (#95: roll modifiers are Boon/Bane, numbers belong to damage). Because attacks always hit and the roll sets the tier, a Boon improves damage by shifting results up the bands with no arithmetic at the table. Potence ladder per 06:59-63 (Boon 1/2/3 = 4d6/5d6/6d6 keep highest three, three steps the ceiling); "double Boon" wording matches ch12:54 and ch05:613. Veto to revert (reverting restores the multi-step damage-tier form and the book-wide anomaly with it). |

| 112 | all (spell and ability cards) | **Do Adept and Master rungs ADD TO the Novice block or REPLACE it?** The book says both in different places: 10:37 describes the card's effects as "Adept and Master additions on top", while most ch11 cards print an Adept line that restates the effect and silently drops the Novice block's conditions. Only the two EXPLICIT contradictions found on the ch11 day were repaired (11:212 "Frozen 2 instead of Restrained", 11:314 Gust's Adept/Master rungs shedding their own advertised Prone). A rung never takes something away, and deepening takes three shapes - *Add* a new effect, *Intensify* a scaled condition (Slowed 10 becomes Slowed 20, Poisoned 1 becomes Poisoned 2, Frozen 1 becomes Frozen 2), or *Tighten* to a stricter condition on the same axis (Prone becomes Restrained, Restrained becomes Stunned). Bruce's constraint: this is NOT a bland carry-everything rule, so the 90-card sweep picks the shape the fiction wants per card rather than copying conditions down mechanically. |

(2) **Folio 365 printed content-free** (measured fill −4.6%, 4 words). Deleting that break restored the folio to 98.6% and took the book to 386 pages, which was the ruling's stated acceptance. Also restored: **Grit was missing from the whole lookup surface** while its own maneuver table charged uses against it, and the derived-stat line regained it. | 161 | 02-character-creation (9 builds) + 16-armor-shields (Roric) | **Every printed hero in the book is wearing gear its own loadout says it must pay for and no ledger in the book pays.** `05:45` states the model (the loadout line's DP figure is "paid from the same Development Points that buy your cards") and the class lines price it (`05:63` Armor ranks 2+3 = 6 DP; `05:113` Armor 4 DP; `05:466` Melee+Armor 5 DP), while `15:82` and `16:41-44` make the rank a hard gate on use. Measured against `origin/main@f33a51a`: **7 of the 9 printed builds are unfunded by 2 to 6 DP, 26 DP of gear permission in total** - Makeva 2 (Odd Armor 2), Sera 4 (Arcanist Melee), Haldra 4 (Shepherd Armor), Marta 2 (Leader Armor), Vaelith 4 (Blade Armor), Pip 4 (Shadow Armor), Gorma 6 (Protector Armor 2+3, she holds rank 1); Lirael and Corwin are clean. The ch16 armor example carries the same defect independently: `16:104` gives Roric **1 Armor rank** and `16:110` has him take **chain mail** (= 3 Armor, printed 60 lines above), and the nine-line deliberation at `16:106-112` never mentions the 6 DP the choice costs. Step 8 (`02:208-210`) is the reason a player reproduces it: the canonical creation path never charges the loadout's ranks at all. | Architect walkthrough W-001 (`docs/design/architect/walkthroughs/2026-09-26-w-001-first-session.md`), 2026-09-26 | **DECIDED 2026-09-26 (architect, cycle 9) - RESOLVED BY ROW 169; this row's own (b) recommendation is retracted in place.** **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-26 (PR #674, main `5c52f12`): every ledger re-derived from the branch file, all nine closing at their named pool, every changed fragment re-read in the 392-page render.**| The ruled direction is not in doubt (#587 parked 2026-09-15: gear ranks are bought at the class's rates from the same pool, "exactly the one the game already charged"), and that row names this exact evidence as its revive trigger: *play shows the level-1 pool is too tight once gear competes with cards for the same ranks*. A Protector who pays 6 of 8 class DP for chain mail keeps **one** card. So the fork is not whether to pay but whether the printed fantasy survives the price: **(a)** the builds pay the ranks and lose 2-6 DP of cards (Gorma's "walking fortress" starts with one ability); **(b)** armor's *entry* requirement drops one step (light 0 / medium 1 / heavy 2) so the light-armor builds are legal free and heavy stays a real purchase; **(c)** loadout gear whose rank the class does not grant is replaced in the build with gear the hero can actually use (leather out, robes in for Sera-style builds). Recommendation: **(b)**, because it is the only one that keeps both the printed builds and the rule which the 2026-09-16 wave installed, and because light armor at 1 Armor was never the load-bearing case for the requirement - the requirement does its work stopping a caster in plate. Veto to any of the three is one word; **do not implement until answered**, because (a) and (c) rewrite nine printed builds. **Cycle 9 ruling (architect, veto-revertible):** the missing level-1 award of 4 DP (row 169) is **exactly the size of the gap this row measured**. With the Level-1 pool at **12 DP** the gear ranks become affordable in **8 of the 9 builds** with no card lost: Makeva 2 (Armor) leaves 10 for her 8 DP of cards, Sera 4 (Melee) leaves 8 for 8, Haldra 4 (Armor) 8 for 8, Marta 2 (Armor) 10 for 8, Vaelith 4 (Armor) 8 for 8, Pip 4 (Armor) 8 for 8, and Lirael and Corwin (0 each, their weapon ranks covered by culture or ancestry grants) leave 12 for 8. **Gorma is the only casualty:** 6 DP of Armor ranks (2 and 3, chain mail) leaves 6 for her 8 DP of cards, so she trims one 2 DP line (Persuasion Novice) and keeps Shield Bash, Perception, Parry and her printed loadout intact. That four builds land on exactly 0 and three on +2/+4 surplus is the evidence that the loadout prices were written against a 12 DP pool and the creation line is what went missing. **This retracts this row's own (b) recommendation** ("armor's entry requirement drops one step: light 0 / medium 1 / heavy 2"): it changes a rule (`16:78-86`'s three-class table, `08:195`) to fund a hole that a missing creation line funds instead, and the smallest-lever rule prefers the line. Also in scope, the same defect in example form: `16:104-112` (Roric) must state the 6 DP the chain-mail choice costs. **Correction, same cycle:** the mechanism itself is already printed (`05:45`: the loadout's remaining ranks are "paid from the same Development Points that buy your cards", and every class line prints its own DP figure), so no Step 8 restatement is needed and none should be added - the only thing missing is the **pool size**, which is row 169. That is also the cleanest proof that the award is the intended reading: the Shepherd's line (`05:202`) is true only against a 12 DP pool and false against 8.|
| 162 | 02-character-creation (9 builds) + 04-ancestries-cultures + 07-skills | **A culture's +1 skill bonus and a purchased Novice rank are the same size and the book never says whether they stack.** `04:112` is the only statement of the bonus ("Each culture grants a *+1 skill bonus*..."): it does not say whether it is subsumed by a purchased rank or adds to it. Every skill costs a flat 2 DP (`07`) and Novice is +1, so the two are indistinguishable in size. **All nine printed heroes spend 2 DP buying the exact skill their own culture grants +1 in** (Makeva Acrobatics, Sera Arcana, Lirael History, Haldra Persuasion, Marta Athletics, Vaelith Deception, Pip Stealth, Gorma Craft, Corwin Survival) = **18 DP of spend that is either additive or dead**, and the nine lines annotate it three different ways: three say "culture bonus already applied" (`02:458`, `02:533`, `02:573`), six say nothing or "favored". Under "subsumed" the book's own builds each teach a wasted purchase; under "additive" the three annotations are false. Either way the fix is one sentence in ch04 plus nine build lines. | Architect walkthrough W-001, 2026-09-26 (measured: 9 of 9 builds, no ledger row existed) | **DECIDED 2026-09-26 (architect, cycle 9) - BALANCE, not a Bruce question; veto to revert.** **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-26 (PR #674, main `5c52f12`): every ledger re-derived from the branch file, all nine closing at their named pool, every changed fragment re-read in the 392-page render.**| Question: does the culture bonus **stack** with a Novice rank (making a culture skill +2 for a hero who spends the 2 DP) or is it **subsumed** (making the 2 DP a waste every build currently demonstrates)? | 180 | 06-core-resolution (the difficulty dial) | **The dial's asymmetry (Trivial +4 to Nearly Impossible -6) read as possible drift.** | Assessment "What matters now" row 4, carried as "needs a ruling". | **DECIDED 2026-09-26 (architect, cycle 12) - BALANCE, not a Bruce question; veto to revert. The asymmetry is load-bearing: a Master specialist reaches mod +5, and a *symmetric* +/-4 dial would leave that hero unfailable on every dialled task (+5 - 4 = +1, P(Weak) still 16.2%), so the -6 end exists precisely to make that hero's task a coin flip (+5 - 6 = -1, P(Weak) 37.5%). The dial is deliberately wider than the dice. Evidence: assessment.md, Assessment 6 re-assessment section. |

No number changes.** (Ruling, not a work order: nothing to build.) The row measured the Weak *band* and reported it as failure. At +5 the Weak band holds exactly one roll of 216 and that roll is (1,1,1), the fumble; the fumble is an automatic failure that overrides the tier (`06:161/189`, `13:358/362`, and unconditional at `01:105`, `07:57-58`, `21:33-35`, `22:28-29`: six homes to one scoping clause). So **P(failure) is flat at 1/216 at +5 and at +6 and never reaches 0**. What retires at +6 is the *ordinary* failure, which is what mastery means, and the bands still discriminate at +5 (Standard 37.0%, Strong 62.5%). |

Measured on the live rows this cycle (Novice 4/6/8: DR 1 soaks 17.6% of an incoming hit, DR 2 35.3%, **DR 3 52.9%**), that is a re-calibration of the whole encounter field, not the refactor the ruling's own invariant demands. **The lock, CORRECTED 2026-09-28b: the CLASS grants its armour card** (Bruce's amendment 1 on the issue, 21:09Z, which the first version of this gate had not read and therefore re-decided - the gate's "granted with the Armor rank for every hero" clause is WITHDRAWN, and amendment 2 generalises it: a class grants ranks and cards, and its signature IS one of them). **What remains undecided and is the gate's live contribution: the tier rule charges twice for a class that is not granted the card and silently imports a level gate** - light 2 DP Novice, medium 4 DP Adept LEVEL 3, heavy 8 DP Master LEVEL 7 - so a Blade who buys Armor 2 at creation has DR 2 at level 1 today and would have it at level 3 under the literal reading, where amendment 1's "pays at its own rates" reads as the ranks only. Reversal word: **"price the cards"** - DR becomes a DP purchase on the card axis, which re-prices the defensive ladder and re-sizes the encounter field and needs its own measurement pass before it ships. **DECIDED (architect, cycle 83, 2026-09-28, veto-revertible): an armour card comes with the Armor ranks it asks for, and carries no level gate of its own.** The three mundane cards (light / medium / heavy) are not a separate DP purchase - a hero who has the ranks has the card, and a class that grants the Armor rank hands over the card with it. That is amendment 1's own cited rule (`15:120` prices armour as the ranks, *"which is why armor is a cost and not a prohibition"*), it is the scale `16:29` already prints (*"A DR grant requires Discipline ranks at least equal to the DR it grants"*), and it is what keeps the Protector's printed loadout (`05:65`: Armor 2 and 3 bought at creation) and `16:96-104`'s level-1 chain-mail example legal. The alternative reading (ranks + the tier's 2/4/8, gated 1/3/7) makes both printed examples illegal and strips DR 2-3 from every armoured Novice build. A **special** suit, magical or otherwise, is the opposite case and is an ordinary bought card: it pays its tier's price and carries that tier's level gate, or magical armour would be free and would dominate the mundane shelf. Scope decided the same way: mount barding (`15:267`) and a monster's own stat object (`20:637`, the Death Knight's soul-vessel armour) are not gear a hero wears and stay as printed; `17`'s entries are cards already (Adamantine Plate, `17:376`) and need no change.
